That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you feel upset about how you look in those photos or mirrors, does it ever affect your eating or cause you to change your food intake?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, sometimes. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you say you cut back after seeing the scale go up, can you tell me more about how much you reduce your food intake and how often this happens?\n\nPatient: I guess it's... most days. So I just stick to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever go without enough food because you're worried about gaining weight or because of how you see your body?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah. I mean, I haven't really been to a doctor lately for anything like that. So, no. Nothing wrong with my thyroid or... whatever else you said.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your overall sense of how you feel about yourself is strongly influenced by how you see your body shape or weight?\n\nPatient: I guess so, yeah. Um... sometimes I'll catch myself in a mirror, or... look at a photo someone took of me next to one of my murals. And if my stomach or my face doesn't look... sharp enough, I guess... it just ruins the whole day. It's hard to feel good about anything else if I don't look right.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your body and the things you do around food ever interfere with your work, relationships, or other important parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I still get my painting jobs done. The work is fine. But... I guess I do skip out on some client meals, or... you know, social things. I just... I don't really want to deal with it. I don't want people looking at what I'm eating, or making comments about how I look. So I just avoid it when I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do anything like exercise a lot or make yourself vomit to try to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I just... I don't eat as much, I guess. But I don't throw up or... spend hours at the gym or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So I just stick to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever go without enough food because you're worried about gaining weight or because of how you see your body?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah. Like I just said a minute ago. If I see the scale move up even a little bit after eating heavy, I just get tense about it. I cut back because I don't want the weight to stick. That's... pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Has your doctor found any medical reasons for your low weight, like a thyroid issue or any digestive problems?\n\nPatient: No... I mean, I haven't really been to a doctor lately for anything like that. So, no. Nothing wrong with my thyroid or... whatever else you said.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your overall sense of how you feel about yourself is strongly influenced by how you see your body shape or weight?\n\nPatient: I guess so, yeah. Um... sometimes I'll catch myself in a mirror, or... look at a photo someone took of me next to one of my murals. And if my stomach or my face doesn't look... sharp enough, I guess... it just ruins the whole day. It's hard to feel good about anything else if I don't look right.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your body and the things you do around food ever interfere with your work, relationships, or other important parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I still get my painting jobs done. The work is fine. But... I guess I do skip out on some client meals, or... you know, social things. I just... I don't really want to deal with it. I don't want people looking at what I'm eating, or making comments about how I look. So I just avoid it when I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do anything like exercise a lot or make yourself vomit to try to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I just... I don't eat as much, I guess. But I don't throw up or... spend hours at the gym or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told that your low weight was a concern before this recent physical, or is this the first time someone has mentioned it to you?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I think... this is the first time. People don't usually say anything to me about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's... lower, I guess. I know the doctor was worried, and... some of my friends have said things too. But I really feel like they're overreacting. I mean... I'm still showing up to my jobs every day. I get all my work done. So... it seems healthy enough to me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how much you currently weigh, and what you think a healthy weight would be for your height and body type?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really step on the scale much. I just know what they told me at the physical. And as for what's healthy... I don't know. Like I said, I feel fine. I'm just trying to stay light on my feet for work.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how much you currently weigh, and whether you’ve intentionally tried to lose weight recently?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know the exact number. Like I said, I don't weigh myself. The doctor at the physical just said it was low. And... no, I'm not trying to lose weight. I just... I work hard. I don't really think about it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you feel fine and that you're just lean, even though others have expressed concern about your weight. Can you tell me more about how you see your body compared to what others might say?\n\nPatient: I don't really focus on what other people say. It's more just... my own thing. Like, if I catch myself in a mirror, or... sometimes people take photos of me next to one of my murals when I finish a job. And I just... I guess I kind of judge the whole day by how I look in them. Like, whether my stomach and my face look sharp enough. If they don't... it just bothers me. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you judge yourself or feel that your worth is tied to how your body looks or how much you weigh?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word 'worth.' But... yeah, like I just said. If I see those photos by my murals, or catch a mirror, and my face or my stomach doesn't look sharp... I just judge the whole day on that. If it's not right, it just ruins the day. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you feel upset about how you look in those photos or mirrors, does it ever affect your eating or cause you to change your food intake?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, sometimes. Like, if I have to go to a client dinner or eat something with bread... I might check the scale. I know I said I don't really weigh myself, but... sometimes I do. And if it goes up even a little, I just get tense. So I'll start cutting back the next day. Just so it doesn't stick. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you say you cut back after seeing the scale go up, can you tell me more about how much you reduce your food intake and how often this happens?\n\nPatient: I guess it's... most days. I usually just keep it to coffee. Maybe a protein bar between paint jobs. And then just a small dinner later. I don't really like stopping to eat a lot anyway. A full meal just... it feels like it would slow me down. So I just stick to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever go without enough food because you're worried about gaining weight or because of how you see your body?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah. Like I just said a minute ago. If I see the scale move up even a little bit after eating heavy, I just get tense about it. I cut back because I don't want the weight to stick. That's... pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Has your doctor found any medical reasons for your low weight, like a thyroid issue or any digestive problems?\n\nPatient: No... I mean, I haven't really been to a doctor lately for anything like that. So, no. Nothing wrong with my thyroid or... whatever else you said.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your overall sense of how you feel about yourself is strongly influenced by how you see your body shape or weight?\n\nPatient: I guess so, yeah. Um... sometimes I'll catch myself in a mirror, or... look at a photo someone took of me next to one of my murals. And if my stomach or my face doesn't look... sharp enough, I guess... it just ruins the whole day. It's hard to feel good about anything else if I don't look right.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your body and the things you do around food ever interfere with your work, relationships, or other important parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I still get my painting jobs done. The work is fine. But... I guess I do skip out on some client meals, or... you know, social things. I just... I don't really want to deal with it. I don't want people looking at what I'm eating, or making comments about how I look. So I just avoid it when I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do anything like exercise a lot or make yourself vomit to try to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I just... I don't eat as much, I guess. But I don't throw up or... spend hours at the gym or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told that your low weight was a concern before this recent physical, or is this the first time someone has mentioned it to you?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I think... this is the first time. People don't usually say anything to me about it.\n\nDoctor: When you choose to eat only coffee, a protein bar, and a small dinner, is it mainly because you’re worried about gaining weight or because you’re afraid of how it might affect your body shape?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's the weight. If I see the scale go up even a little bit... like after a client dinner, or if I eat something with bread... I just get tense. So I cut back the next day, just to make sure it doesn't stick. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately how much you weigh now, and about your height, so we can get a sense of your body mass index?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know my exact height. Just average, I guess. And for my weight... I'm not sure what the exact number is right now. I just... I just keep an eye on it to make sure it doesn't go up.\n\nDoctor: Can you please provide your current weight in pounds and your height in feet and inches so we can better understand your body mass index?\n\nPatient: I... I just told you, I don't really know. I don't measure myself. I'm just... average height. And the weight, I just... I don't focus on the exact number, I just know when it feels wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you please tell me your current weight in pounds and your height in feet and inches so we can calculate your body mass index?\n\nPatient: I... look, I'm not trying to be difficult, I just... I don't know the exact numbers. I don't step on a scale and write it down. I'm maybe five foot ten, or somewhere around there. And my weight... I don't know, maybe 130? 135? I just... I don't keep track of the exact pounds. I just go by how my clothes fit. That's all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I mean, I haven't really been to a doctor lately for anything like that. So, no.
